# CHANGELOG.md
Its entries dated before 2026-10-12 stand, whole and unchanged, in `docs/archive/CHANGELOG-to-2026-10-11.md`, read with this file as one record.

The prior text of every clean edit to a spec. `changelog-reconciles` reads the git history and fails any commit that deleted a line from a spec without changing this file.

Specs are `CLAUDE.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.html`, `SCHEMA.md`, `BUILD_PLAN.md` and `RUNBOOK.md`, and the four files under `.claude/rules/`, which carry `CLAUDE.md`'s own text and are edited on the same terms. Records correct themselves with new dated entries and do not appear here.

Nothing in the corpus is struck through. A spec is edited cleanly and what it said before is written down here, with the decision that authorised the change or the defect it repairs.

## Entry format

```
### YYYY-MM-DD - <file> - <what changed>
Authorised by: <a decision name, cited exactly>   OR
Corrects: <the defect, in one line, and how it was found>
Was:
> the prior text, verbatim
Now:
> the replacing text, or a note that the passage was removed
Why: one or two sentences
```

An entry names one or the other and never neither. A change that alters what the corpus decides cites the decision that authorises it; a change that repairs a spec which was wrong when written names the defect, because there was no decision to make and inventing one to fill the field would make the field unreadable.

---

### 2026-10-22 - RUNBOOK.md - pin 4 written under LM Studio's newest runtime carries none of pin 3's readings
Authorised by: A new reading version carries each reading whose request it would send unchanged and reads the rest
Was:
> ... `--pin` then writes pin 4, and since pin 3 differs from it in the fit's rule alone it carries, in the same write, pin 3's reading of every release ... Its row on the run log under `local-run` carries the same counts.
Now:
> ... `--pin` then writes pin 4, and where pin 3 differs from it in the fit's rule alone it carries, in the same write, pin 3's reading of every release ... Its row on the run log under `local-run` carries the same counts. On the operator's ruling of 2026-10-22 pin 4 is written under LM Studio's newest runtime, the CUDA 12 runtime 2.62.1 selected in the place of pin 3's 2.56.0 with `lms runtime select` and the model unloaded before `--pin`, so pin 4 carries none of pin 3's readings and prints no counts, and the history read reads every release under it, about three days at 250 W.
Why: the operator ruled on 2026-10-22 that the history read runs on LM Studio's newest runtime, and a version carries a reading only from one whose runtime it shares.

> What the variants did differently: a select choosing one family at a time kept in the hash with its card held in place; a family with no variant saying so; a row its live or provisional rule and a row a variant by its number: what it changes in plain words; its trades; those it shared with the live rule and those it alone took; its return so far with its record's too-few label; its verdict beside its name with its full words under the pointer; and beneath the table the trades only a variant took: the stock and the variant and when it was bought and at what and whether it is open or how it ended with its result (see: A variant's picks are shown on its card when chosen and its results on paper trading beside its verdict, never on Tonight)
>
> Rule scorecard: a select choosing one rule at a time kept in the hash with its card held in place; the rule's family and its standing with its words and the version it counts under the pointer and a link to its trade log; its history and its record since it went live drawn as two halves side by side and never added together, one beneath the other on a phone; and its variants folding out beneath under one press with each one's verdict on history and the years it was better than its rule of those the test counted and its progress since it went live (see: A rule's scorecard draws its test years and its record since it went live as two halves, and never adds them together)
>
> An account's test years verdict: the scorecard's half on history over the test years the newest per-rule test on the index read; under the question Did it beat the easy alternative the years it was better of those the test counted and a box a test year saying better or worse with that year's edge a unit, a year not yet complete marked so; under the question Could that just be luck a scale in three zones with the rule's mark on it: easily luck at an adjusted p at or over the level a run is held to; maybe luck between the bar and that level; real at or under the bar; in words how often a rule picking at random would do as well, about so many times in a hundred or under one in a hundred about one in so many; and that to count as real it needs to be rarer than one in the bar's reciprocal; the adjusted p against the bar under the pointer; for its live or provisional rule the warning that its settings were tuned on these years so it should be expected to look worse from here on; and a rule not tested yet or not testable saying so with its verdict in words (see: Every registered rule is tested each month on the test years, each variant against its live rule and each live rule against its benchmark)
>
> An account's record since registration: the scorecard's half since it went live: its decided trades against the count the test judges a swing family on or a book's months against its count of months as a line and a bar from the day it counts from; the mean edge of its decided trades whose comparison is read, labelled not evidence yet until the count is met; a provisional rule saying it keeps no record until its family is frozen or a setting is approved; its return drawn on its tile beside the index's fund and in the chart and never in its verdict's place (see: A rule's scorecard draws its test years and its record since it went live as two halves, and never adds them together)
>
> Your own trades: the operator's own trades on the index as an account apart from the system's: their count and those decided in one line; the account's return and its worst drop and its return less its fund's; the day it starts; a link to its trade log; or that none is taken, saying how a trade is taken and that no test reads them
>
> The lines beneath: beneath what the system bought the line that each entry is bought at the open of the session after the night that listed it (see: A trade's entry is priced at the next session's open by a fill step of its own, and no rule's version moves because of it); beneath the variants' table that a variant is adopted only through the monthly test and the operator's approval on the Loop page
>
> The test years verdict and the record since registration answer different questions and are drawn apart, as the scorecard's two halves: the verdict is the rule over the ledger's years in the newest monthly per-rule test, read as soon as one exists under that test's own screens, and the record is the account's own trades since the rule was registered, which reads nothing until it holds the count the test judges a family on. A handful of trades looks like evidence and is not, so the record carries its not evidence yet label until then, and its return is drawn on its tile and in the chart and never in the verdict's place (see: A rule's scorecard draws its test years and its record since it went live as two halves, and never adds them together).
>
> From the 19.4 correction of 2026-10-13 ... The trades of earlier versions stay stored, folded beneath what the system bought and in the account's trade log, and are in none of its figures. ...
>
> A paragraph added: From the 19.4 correction of 2026-10-22, on the operator's ruling redesigning the page from a mockup they approved as phase 19's layout had left it hard to read, the page is drawn from the top in the order its rows state, a part a card, each choice a part offers kept in the link. Every figure a part draws beside the walk is worked by the core's one function with it and the page computes none: ... Adjusted p values, version codes and registration days stand under the pointer and not in the text.
>
> Figure 15.6 redrawn as the page from the top in six parts: what the page is with the account's line and its press; a tile a family's live or provisional rule beside the fund's with the chart beneath; what the system bought with its tabs, its chips, a trade's row and the stocks not bought; what the variants did differently; a rule's scorecard in two halves; and your own trades, every figure drawn as n or n.n rather than any night's; its key naming each of the six as the figure draws it, its caption kept.
Why: the operator ruled on 2026-10-22, from a mockup they approved, that paper trading be redrawn top to bottom as phase 19's redesign of the screens had left it hard to read, its figures read from the store and every new one worked by the core's one function; the table of every account, its order by verdict and then return and its years' paired bars give way to the tiles, what the system bought, the variants' part and the scorecard.

### 2026-10-18 - RUNBOOK.md - the history read runs around the clock with the card's power held at 250 W
Authorised by: Phase 20 is built whole in the order its dependencies set with the sign-offs of phases 18 and 19 after it, spending no money and retiring nothing on a result
Was:
> ... It needs LM Studio running and holding the model, and loads it where it is not loaded. 20.0 measured 3.48 seconds a release, so the history's 38,400 releases take about 37 hours of the GPU; run from after the night's copy, about 21:30 in New York, to `--until 07:00`, a weeknight gives about nine and a half hours, about four nights, and a weekend day the hours between its slots.
Now:
> ... It needs LM Studio running and holding the model, and loads it where it is not loaded. It runs around the clock with the card's power held at 250 W (see: Phase 20 is built whole in the order its dependencies set with the sign-offs of phases 18 and 19 after it, spending no money and retiring nothing on a result): set the limit first from a prompt run as administrator with `nvidia-smi -pl 250`, which the machine forgets when it restarts, read it back with `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=power.limit,temperature.gpu --format=csv`, and stop the read where the card's core reaches 72 degrees. Started after the morning's merges with `--until 07:00`, it reads through the day and the night, keeping clear of the slots and the night by itself, and stops at 07:00 in New York the next morning for the day's merges. Pin 2's read took 4.63 seconds a release over its first 1,181 with the card at its limit of 390 W, and about 5.2 held at 250 W, about 13 per cent slower a token, so the history's 45,328 releases take about 65 hours of the GPU, about three days.
Why: the same ruling; the times are pin 2's read as measured over the 45,328 releases the history fetch stored, in place of 20.0's estimate over 38,400.

> Rules choose the stocks, and a language model writes only the reports. Every stock a list draws, and the order it draws them in, comes from rules over stored prices, filings, counts and dates, and no language model's output decides, filters, ranks or orders a pick, a family, a variant, a gate or a record. A language model writes the research reports, and labels the news for the reader and for the reports alone. The reason is the test the picks are held to: a rule can be replayed over the stored history as each night stood and scored, and gives the same answer when it is run again, where a language model's judgment can be neither replayed as it stood nor reproduced. A statistical model fitted by code to the ledger passes that test, so it is a rule, and it orders a list only as a rule's registered setting the operator approved (see: Rules choose the stocks, and a language model writes only the reports) (see: A fitted statistical model is a rule)
Now:
> ... tonight's list is chosen on the chart and the plan alone, with no model's judgment in the decision.
> Rules choose the stocks, and no model judges a stock. Every stock a list draws, and the order it draws them in, comes from rules over stored prices, filings, counts and dates, and over the stored readings a pinned local model gave of a member's results release with its names and dates removed. No other model's output decides, filters, ranks or orders a pick, a family, a variant, a gate or a record: a language model writes the research reports, and labels the news for the reader and for the reports alone. ... A local model's reading passes it only pinned, its model's file, its runtime and its prompt fixed and checked on every run at temperature zero with a fixed seed, so it reads a document the same way again; it answers closed questions alone, each a word code turns into a reading, and is asked for no number, no date and no estimate. A language model's judgment can be neither replayed as it stood nor reproduced. ... (see: Rules choose the stocks from stored data and a pinned local model's readings of documents with their names removed, and no model judges a stock) (see: A fitted statistical model is a rule)
> Added without replacing anything: section 20's row for phase 20.
Why: the operator ruled with phase 20's brief that section 1's boundary be amended to match the narrowed decision, and section 20 carries one row for every phase the plan holds.

### 2026-10-13 - docs/SCHEMA.md - the read surface reads a holding's close a look-back before the night from the kept bars

Now:

No night reads it: it is a setup's path, replayed from the anchor the setup stores (see: The bars the fetcher drops are kept in a table of their own that no night reads, and a setup is stored as its anchor). From 19.3 the read surface reads a heavyweight holding's close a look-back before the night from it where the store's year holds one session fewer, only where no refetch has written the stock's year again since the session was kept (see: Paper trading's accounts and a heavyweight's standing tonight are worked by one function in the core when a page is read).
Why: the store keeps 251 sessions on most nights, one short of a 251-session look-back's first close, so the standing read off the store's year alone would read nothing; a refetch since the drop puts the year on another basis, and then the standing reads nothing.

### 2026-10-13 - docs/ARCHITECTURE.html - section 15.6's colours, the night status mark and the Run page's status sentence follow the green and the red of 19.2

Text in either is drawn in its ink form. (see: Support and resistance own two hues, which also rule the name page's parts that buy and sell and nothing else)</p> <h4>A rise and a fall have hues of their own, on the name page alone</h4> <p>On the name page and its exported file a figure that rose is drawn in indigo and one that fell in plum, each with its sign always written beside it, and a caution in amber with the word written beside it. The rise and the fall are drawn by the name page's rules alone, because they sit close to the Run page's status blue and waiting violet, and the failure's red marks no rise or fall. (see: A figure that rose or fell is drawn in a hue of its own on the name page and its export alone)</p> <h4>Hue is never the only channel</h4> <p>Every band carries its role in words as well as its colour, every change its sign and every caution its word. ...
> 15.6: ... the level, rise, fall and caution inks read so on their own fills as well.
> 15.6: <h4>Status is drawn in blues, a wait in violet and a failure in red</h4> <p>... a night not run yet, which is waiting, in violet; and a night that stopped, was left unfinished or never ran in red, which means a failure and nothing else, never a fall in price. ... (see: Status is drawn in blues with violet for a wait and red for a failure alone)</p>
> 15.6: ... the two hues for a level below and above the price and the parts that buy and sell, the rise, the fall and the caution, ... the status blues, violet and red, and four steps of one neutral for more or less.
> 15.5 Night status: ... a clock in violet is a night not run yet or waiting to try again ... (see: Status is drawn in blues with violet for a wait and red for a failure alone).

Now:

### 2026-10-13 - docs/BUILD_PLAN.md - 19.2's push view is in typical moves, from a setup at the means to the stock's score
Corrects: 19.2's text named the push view's unit as risks, where the learned score is fitted on each setup's edge in typical moves, found while building the chart against the score's own arithmetic.
Was:
> each heading's share of the score, its weights times the stock's standardised readings summed by heading, in risks, green where it pushes up and red where it pushes down.
Now:
> each heading's share of the score, its weights times the stock's standardised readings summed by heading, in typical moves, the unit the score is learned in, green where it pushes up and red where it pushes down, the pushes taking the score of a setup at the means the score learned on to the stock's own.
Why: a push is a weight times a reading standardised about its mean, so the pushes sum to the score less the score of a setup at the means, and only with that score beside them do they add up to the stock's own.

### 2026-10-12 - docs/ARCHITECTURE.html, docs/SCHEMA.md, docs/RUNBOOK.md, docs/BUILD_PLAN.md - the citations of the old loop's seven decisions move to the four decisions superseding them
Decision: A registered rule's looks and its futility are monitoring, and a change is adopted only by the operator's approval of a tested proposal
Was:
> (see: Adding a candidate later restarts the clock)
> (see: A candidate's verdict is read only at looks fixed when it is registered, with each look's boundary found over every sign vector its blocks allow)
> (see: A candidate is retired by a futility guideline at its first two looks or by reaching its last look)
> (see: A shape acceptance restarts the live filter's edge clock, and after one acceptance while the list is live each further one states the blocks it restarts)
> (see: The shape proposer moves one setting a gate, nearest first, and never applies what it proposes)
> (see: The variations a sweep explores are exploration, and count toward no correction of the live family)
> (see: Condition thresholds are calibrated from your own nights, not from a backfill)
Now:
> (see: A registration restarts only the registered rule's own record)
> (see: A registered rule's looks and its futility are monitoring, and a change is adopted only by the operator's approval of a tested proposal), for the looks and for the futility guideline
> (see: The shape clock proposes moves tested like any proposal, and an approved move opens the next filter version restarting the live filter's record), for both shape decisions
> (see: History reaches a list only through a test on years a proposal never saw and the operator's approval), for the sweep's variations and the thresholds' calibration
Why: 17.14 moves every decision of the old loop to Previously decided on the operator's answer of 2026-10-12, "Full, pullback included", so every citation in the specs moves with it: in the architecture's catalogue, sections 13, 14, 15 and 17, the schema's filter versions and shape proposals, the runbook's shape and candidate sections, and the build plan's phase 10 and 12 text and two operating rows. ### 2026-10-12 - docs/RUNBOOK.md - the shape command's section, the trade autopsy, the Loop page's presses and promoting a candidate state 17.14
Decision: The shape clock proposes moves tested like any proposal, and an approved move opens the next filter version restarting the live filter's record
Was:
> ### Opening the swing filter's version, and a shape proposal accepted or rejected
> The filter's settings move only through this command, run by the operator from the repository root, and a night never runs it (see: ...). ...
> **A shape proposal** is written by the night ... and, beside it, the non-empty blocks the live filter's clock has run (see: ...). Accept it by its number, or reject it with the reason:
> dotnet run --project src/EquityBrief.Worker -- shape --accept 1
> A rejection writes the reason on the proposal's row and changes nothing else, and the next proposal for the version waits on sixty more ordinary nights. An acceptance opens the proposal's settings as the next version; a proposal written for a version no longer open is refused. Before the swing family registers, an acceptance restarts nothing. Once the live filter's candidate stands registered, an acceptance also retires it and registers the accepted settings in the same write, which restarts its edge clock: the first such acceptance states nothing more, and every later one must state the blocks the run page draws beside the proposal, and is refused with nothing changed without them or at any other count:
> dotnet run --project src/EquityBrief.Worker -- shape --accept 2 --restarts 3
> ... and writes them to `loop_finding`. The S&P 500's pullback is the swing filter's walk and is proposed no exit.
> ... Approve is not offered on the S&P 500's pullback, whose rule changes through its filter's versions, nor on a sector heavyweights book, which changes by a freeze alone; the page says why beside each.
> ... The first look cannot promote: the level it releases is below the smallest p-value eight blocks can produce, so it can only retire.
Now:
> ### Opening the swing filter's version, and a shape proposal's moves approved or the proposal rejected
> The filter's settings move through this command, run by the operator from the repository root, and from 17.14 through a shape move approved on the Loop page, which the night opens as the next version (see: ...). ...
> **A shape proposal** is written by the night ... the non-empty blocks approving one of its moves restarts. From 17.14 the command accepts no proposal: each of its moves is a proposal of the next tester run on the S&P 500, ... Approve a move there, and the next night ... opens the next version with the move made ...
> A rejection writes the reason on the proposal's row and changes nothing else, so its moves are tested no more, ... Settings you rule with `--settings` still open a version as a ruling: ...
> dotnet run --project src/EquityBrief.Worker -- shape --settings dryUpCeiling=1.25 --restarts 3 --evidence "the ruling of the day"
> ... and writes them to `loop_finding`. From 17.14 the S&P 500's pullback is walked as the swing filter at its open version's settings, read by value, and proposed exits as every family is, and each move of the newest shape proposal written for that version is tested in the same run (see: ...).
> ... Approve is not offered on a sector heavyweights book, which changes by a freeze alone; the page says why beside it. From 17.14 the S&P 500's pullback takes a change of its hooks, ... and a shape move, which the night opens as the filter's next version (see: ...).
> ... so it can only retire. From 17.14 what a look reads is monitoring and changes nothing by itself: the steps below are yours to take on its evidence, and a change to how a swing family picks reaches its list only as a proposal the tester passed and you approved on the Loop page.
Why: the shape command accepts no proposal, a move is opened by the night once approved, the S&P 500's pullback is proposed exits and takes approvals, and a look's crossing is monitoring.
